I mean, the divorce was stressful, obviously, but... I wouldn't say I feel hopeless or down. I'm still doing my usual stuff. I go to work, I'm still hitting my sales targets, and I haven't lost interest in any of that. I feel pretty normal in that regard, just... focused, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or eating habits recently, especially around the time your weight started dropping?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose my habits have changed a bit. Most days I just keep it to coffee in the morning. Then maybe I'll grab a protein bar between my sales calls. At night, I stick to something pretty small, like... just some grilled chicken without any rice or sides. \n\nIt's just that, lately, if I sit down and start eating a full, heavy meal, it kind of feels like I messed up the whole day. So keeping it light and strict just works better for me right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself being overly concerned about gaining weight or about the shape of your body, even if others might not see a reason for concern?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I do. It's just... if I step on the scale and it's even a pound higher after a client dinner, or maybe a weekend out in Miami, I get this immediate panic. It really feels like I'm losing control of things. So right away, I start planning exactly how I'm going to cut back the next day to fix it. I just don't want to lose the edge I've gotten.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit, used laxatives or diuretics, or exercised excessively to control your weight or shape?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do any of that. I don't make myself throw up, and I don't take laxatives or anything like that. And as for exercise, I mean, I go for a run sometimes, but nothing crazy or excessive. I just stick to controlling what I eat.\n\nDoctor: Has the weight loss been something you've actively tried to achieve, or has it happened without you specifically trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I think I look fine, but they're the ones saying it's not a healthy weight.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me a rough estimate of how long it's been since you started noticing the weight loss, and how long it's been since you first became aware that others were concerned about how thin you've become?\n\nPatient: I don't really have an exact timeframe for you. It feels like my sister and the people at work just started making comments somewhat recently, but I couldn't give you a specific number of weeks or months. My weight has kept dropping since my divorce, but... yeah, they were pretty insistent that I come in and talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you used to enjoy lately?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, the divorce was stressful, obviously, but... I wouldn't say I feel hopeless or down. I'm still doing my usual stuff. I go to work, I'm still hitting my sales targets, and I haven't lost interest in any of that. I feel pretty normal in that regard, just... focused, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or eating habits recently, especially around the time your weight started dropping?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose my habits have changed a bit. Most days I just keep it to coffee in the morning. Then maybe I'll grab a protein bar between my sales calls. At night, I stick to something pretty small, like... just some grilled chicken without any rice or sides. \n\nIt's just that, lately, if I sit down and start eating a full, heavy meal, it kind of feels like I messed up the whole day. So keeping it light and strict just works better for me right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself being overly concerned about gaining weight or about the shape of your body, even if others might not see a reason for concern?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I do. It's just... if I step on the scale and it's even a pound higher after a client dinner, or maybe a weekend out in Miami, I get this immediate panic. It really feels like I'm losing control of things. So right away, I start planning exactly how I'm going to cut back the next day to fix it. I just don't want to lose the edge I've gotten.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit, used laxatives or diuretics, or exercised excessively to control your weight or shape?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do any of that. I don't make myself throw up, and I don't take laxatives or anything like that. And as for exercise, I mean, I go for a run sometimes, but nothing crazy or excessive. I just stick to controlling what I eat.\n\nDoctor: Has the weight loss been something you've actively tried to achieve, or has it happened without you specifically trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I don't think I ever sat down and made it a specific goal to lose a bunch of weight, if that's what you mean. \n\nIt just... it kind of happened on its own once I started changing how I eat. I really just wanted to be more disciplined and feel sharper for work. So I got a lot stricter with my meals, and the weight loss just sort of followed along with that. I didn't necessarily plan for it to drop this much.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me approximately how much weight you've lost since your divorce, and how that compares to what you would consider a normal or healthy weight for yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm honestly only here because HR at work and my sister keep getting on my case, saying I've gotten too thin. Since the divorce, my weight has just kept dropping. I don't really know the exact number of pounds, but... I mean, from my perspective, I just see that my suits fit a lot sharper now. I think I look fine, but they're the ones saying it's not a healthy weight.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me a rough estimate of how long it's been since you started noticing the weight loss, and how long it's been since you first became aware that others were concerned about how thin you've become?\n\nPatient: I don't really have an exact timeframe for you. It's hard to say exactly when I first noticed it... I mean, it was just a gradual thing after the divorce. I wasn't really tracking the days or anything like that. \n\nAnd as for when everyone else started getting on my case... I'm not entirely sure about that either. It feels like my sister and the people at work just started making comments somewhat recently, but I couldn't give you a specific number of weeks or months. I just haven't really been paying that close attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me approximately what you weigh now compared to what you weighed before the divorce, and how tall are you?\n\nPatient: I don't actually own a scale, so I couldn't give you a specific number for what I weigh right now. And I really don't know what I weighed before the divorce, either. I just wasn't keeping track of it back then. Like I mentioned, I mostly just go by how my suits fit. \n\nAs for my height... I mean, I haven't been measured at a doctor's office in years, so I don't have an exact number for you there, either. I'm just not really focused on the numbers.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me a rough estimate of how many months ago you started noticing the weight loss?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I really don't know. The divorce was a while ago, and the weight loss just kind of happened over time after that. I wasn't marking it on a calendar or anything, so I can't give you a number of months. I just started noticing my clothes fitting differently at some point, but I couldn't tell you when that started.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you've lost more than 10% of your body weight in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't tell you. I mean, since I don't know what I weighed to begin with, or what I weigh right now... I really have no way of knowing if it's been ten percent. I just know my suits are a lot looser than they used to be, but as for an actual percentage, I just don't have those numbers to give you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told by a medical professional that your weight is dangerously low or that you are underweight based on medical standards?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Like I mentioned earlier, I haven't actually been to a doctor's office in years, so... no, I've never had a medical professional tell me my weight is dangerously low or anything like that. I just haven't been in a position for anyone to say that to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your weight loss was something that just happened without you really trying, or was it more of a result of your efforts to stay disciplined with your eating habits?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's definitely a result of trying to stay disciplined. And then for dinner, just something small, like some grilled chicken without any rice. It's just... well, once I start eating a full meal, it feels like I completely messed up the whole day. So, yeah, I'm definitely controlling it on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the changes in your eating habits and the weight loss have been going on for more or less than three months?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure exactly how long it's been. I haven't been keeping track of the calendar or anything like that. It's just been my routine for a while now, but whether it's been more or less than three months... I honestly couldn't give you a straight answer on that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your changes in eating habits and the weight loss have been going on for at least six months?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I really haven't been keeping track of the time. It could be six months, it could be less... I just don't know for sure. It's just been my normal routine for a while.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
